chapterRotational Motion high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the radii of gyration from the moments of inertia of a solid sphere (2/5 MR^2) and a thin hollow sphere (2/3 MR^2), which is rotational motion.
subtopic 1Radius of Gyration
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on comparing the radii of gyration k = sqrt(I/M) of a solid sphere and a thin hollow sphere, which is taught under radius of gyration.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The ratio of radius of gyration of a solid sphere of mass M and radius R about its own axis to the radius of gyration of the thin hollow sphere of same mass and radius about its axis is
  1. 5 : 3
  2. 2 : 5
  3. 5 : 2
  4. 3 : 5
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · medium
  1. For a solid sphere, I1 = 25MR2, so k1 = R25.
  2. For a thin hollow sphere, I2 = 23MR2, so k2 = R23.
  3. k1k2 = 2/52/3 = 35 = 3 : 5.
  4. No option shows 3 : 5; option 4 (3 : 5) has the right numbers and is the intended answer if the square roots were lost.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 mediumagree
difficultymedium · 2 steps
stepsRecall the moments of inertia of a solid sphere (2/5 MR2) and a thin hollow sphere (2/3 MR2) about a diameter → Use I = MK2 to express each radius of gyration and take their ratio (a square root of the ratio of the moments of inertia)
why it is trickyThe work is two standard moment-of-inertia recalls followed by converting them to radii of gyration via I = MK2 and forming the ratio.
the catchAll four printed options are plain integer ratios with no square root, while the radius-of-gyration ratio involves a square root; a candidate who stops at the ratio of moments of inertia finds a matching option, and one who completes the square root finds none printed exactly.

Q2Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)
branchnot applicable (Physics has no branches)
chapterModern Physics high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the photoelectric condition that emission occurs only when photon energy exceeds the work function (2.20 eV > 2.14 eV for Cs only), which is the photoelectric effect.
subtopic 1Photoelectric Effect
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the photoelectric threshold condition that photoemission occurs only when incident photon energy (2.20 eV) exceeds the surface work function, which only Cs (2.14 eV) satisfies.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The work functions of Caesium (Cs), Potassium (K) and Sodium (Na) are 2.14 eV, 2.30 eV and 2.75 eV respectively. If incident electromagnetic radiation has an incident energy of 2.20 eV, which of these photosensitive surfaces may emit photoelectrons?
  1. Both Na and K
  2. K only
  3. Na only
  4. Cs only
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Photoelectrons are emitted only if Ephoton ≥ φ.
  2. Cs: φ = 2.14 eV < 2.20 eV, so Cs emits.
  3. K: φ = 2.30 eV > 2.20 eV, so K does not emit.
  4. Na: φ = 2.75 eV > 2.20 eV, so Na does not emit.
  5. Only Cs emits, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the photoelectric condition that emission requires photon energy at least equal to the work function, and compare 2.20 eV against each of the three given work functions
why it is trickyThe work is a single recalled condition (E >= work function) applied as a direct numerical comparison of 2.20 eV with three stated values; no calculation is needed.

Q3Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)
branchnot applicable (Physics has no branches)
chapterMechanical Properties of Fluids high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on surface energy = surface tension x increase in area for a bubble with two surfaces (W = 8*pi*r^2*T = 3.01e-4 J), which is taught under surface tension and surface energy.
subtopic 1Surface Tension and Surface Energy
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer is the surface energy W = T x 2(4*pi*r^2) for the two surfaces of a soap bubble, which is taught under surface tension and surface energy.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The amount of energy required to form a soap bubble of radius 2 cm from a soap solution is nearly (surface tension of soap solution = 0.03 N m-1)
  1. 5.06 × 10-4 J
  2. 3.01 × 10-4 J
  3. 50.1 × 10-4 J
  4. 30.16 × 10-4 J
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. A soap bubble has two free surfaces, so the area created is A = 2 × 4π r2 = 8π r2.
  2. r = 2 cm = 0.02 m, so r2 = 4 × 10-4 m2.
  3. A = 8π × 4 × 10-4 = 32π × 10-4 m2 ≈ 1.005 × 10-2 m2.
  4. W = T A = 0.03 × 1.005 × 10-2 ≈ 3.016 × 10-4 J.
  5. 3.01 × 10-4 J is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that a soap bubble has two free surfaces (inner and outer), so the new surface area created is 2 x 4*π*r2 → Convert radius 2 cm to 0.02 m and compute the total area 8*π*r2 → Multiply surface tension by the total area created (W = T x δ A) and compute the numerical value in the 10-4 J range
why it is trickyThe work is a recall of W = T x (area created) plus the application that a bubble has two surfaces, followed by a cm-to-m conversion and arithmetic.
the catchOptions differ by a factor of ten (3.01 vs 30.16 x 10^-4 J), so a slip in the cm-to-m squared conversion lands on a printed option.

subtopic 1Colour Code of Carbon Resistors
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the carbon resistor colour code, where the third band is the multiplier (10^3 for 22000 ohm = 22 x 10^3), which is orange.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Resistance of a carbon resistor determined from colour codes is (22000 ± 5%) Ω. The colour of third band must be
  1. Green
  2. Orange
  3. Yellow
  4. Red
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Write the value as two significant digits times a power of ten: 22000 Ω = 22 × 103 Ω.
  2. The first two bands give the digits 2, 2, and the third band gives the multiplier 103.
  3. In the colour code, multiplier 3 is orange, so the third band is orange, option 2.

Q5Physics
subjectPhysics

chapterAlternating Current high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the resonant frequency of a series LCR circuit, f = 1/(2π√(LC)) = 1.59 kHz, which is taught in Alternating Current.
subtopic 1Resonance
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer uses the resonant frequency formula f = 1/(2π√(LC)) of a series LCR circuit, giving 1.59 kHz.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
In a series LCR circuit, the inductance L is 10 mH, capacitance C is 1 μF and resistance R is 100 Ω. The frequency at which resonance occurs is
  1. 15.9 kHz
  2. 1.59 rad/s
  3. 1.59 kHz
  4. 15.9 rad/s
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. Resonance in a series LCR circuit occurs at ω0 = 1LC, independent of R.
  2. LC = (10 × 10-3 H)(1 × 10-6 F) = 10-8 s2.
  3. LC = 10-4 s, so ω0 = 104 rad/s.
  4. f0 = ω02π = 1042π ≈ 1591.5 Hz = 1.59 kHz, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
33 highagree
difficultymedium · 2 steps
stepsRecall the resonance condition f = 1/(2*π*LC) and recognise that R plays no role → Convert L = 10 mH and C = 1 uF to SI, evaluate LC = 1e-4 s and divide by 2*π to get the frequency in kHz
why it is trickyOne formula recall followed by a substitution that needs SI conversion of mH and uF and a power-of-ten evaluation, while ignoring the given resistance.
the catchR = 100 ohm is a distractor value, options mix Hz and rad/s units, and two options differ only by a factor of ten, so a slip in the mH/uF conversion lands on a listed option.

chapterElectromagnetic Waves high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the relation B0 = E0/c for a plane electromagnetic wave in free space, giving 48/(3x10^8) = 1.6x10^-7 T.
subtopic 1Relation Between E, B, and Speed of Light
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer uses B0 = E0/c = 48/(3x10^8) = 1.6x10^-7 T, the relation between E, B and the speed of light.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
In a plane electromagnetic wave travelling in free space, the electric field component oscillates sinusoidally at a frequency of 2.0 × 1010 Hz and amplitude 48 V m-1. Then the amplitude of oscillating magnetic field is (Speed of light in free space = 3 × 108 m s-1)
  1. 1.6 × 10-8 T
  2. 1.6 × 10-7 T
  3. 1.6 × 10-6 T
  4. 1.6 × 10-9 T
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. For a plane EM wave in free space the field amplitudes are related by B0 = E0c.
  2. B0 = 48 V m-13 × 108 m s-1 = 16 × 10-8 T.
  3. 16 × 10-8 T = 1.6 × 10-7 T, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall B0 = E0/c for a plane EM wave in free space and substitute E0 = 48 V/m, c = 3e8 m/s
why it is trickyA single substitution of the given field amplitude and speed of light into the stated relation B0 = E0/c; the frequency is not needed.

Given below are two statements:
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below.
Statement IPhotovoltaic devices can convert optical radiation into electricity.
Statement IIZener diode is designed to operate under reverse bias in breakdown region.
  1. Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
  2. Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
  3. Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
  4. Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

  1. Statement I: a photovoltaic device such as a solar cell generates an emf when optical radiation falls on it, so it converts light into electricity; Statement I is correct.
  2. Statement II: a Zener diode is heavily doped and is designed to operate under reverse bias in the breakdown region (used as a voltage regulator); Statement II is correct.
  3. Both statements are correct, which is option 4.

Q9Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)
branchnot applicable (Physics has no branches)
chapterElectrostatics high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on interpreting zero net electric flux through a closed surface (Gauss's law / electric flux), meaning flux lines entering equal those leaving.
subtopic 1Electric Flux
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer rests on interpreting zero net electric flux through a closed surface as equal numbers of field lines entering and leaving it.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
If ∮s →E · →dS = 0 over a surface, then
  1. The magnitude of electric field on the surface is constant
  2. All the charges must necessarily be inside the surface
  3. The electric field inside the surface is necessarily uniform
  4. The number of flux lines entering the surface must be equal to the number of flux lines leaving it
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. By Gauss's law, ∮s →E· d→S = qencε0.
  2. Given the flux is 0, so qenc = 0; charges may be outside, so option 2 is wrong.
  3. Zero net flux does not require |→E| constant on the surface or →E uniform inside, so options 1 and 3 are wrong.
  4. Net flux = Φout - Φin = 0 ⇒ Φin = Φout: lines entering equal lines leaving, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall Gauss's law meaning: zero net flux through a closed surface means net enclosed charge is zero and flux entering equals flux leaving, then eliminate the field-magnitude/uniformity/charge-location options by that same recall
why it is trickyThe work is a single conceptual recall of what zero net electric flux through a closed surface implies, with the other options rejected directly from that recall.

Q10Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)
branchnot applicable (Physics has no branches)
chapterCurrent Electricity high confidence
why this chapterZero galvanometer deflection means a balanced Wheatstone bridge, and R follows from the bridge balance condition taught in Current Electricity.
subtopic 1Kirchhoff's Laws of Current Distribution
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicWith no current through G, the potential drop across R must equal the 2 V cell, so applying Kirchhoff's loop law to the 10 V-400 ohm-R loop gives 10R/(400+R) = 2, i.e. R = 100 ohm.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
If the galvanometer G does not show any deflection in the circuit shown, the value of R is given by
  1. 50 Ω
  2. 100 Ω
  3. 400 Ω
  4. 200 Ω
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. With no deflection in G, no current flows through the 2 V branch, so the potential difference across R equals 2 V.
  2. The 10 V cell then drives current only through 400 Ω and R in series: I = 10400 + R.
  3. VR = IR = 10R400 + R = 2.
  4. 10R = 800 + 2R ⇒ 8R = 800 ⇒ R = 100 Ω, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecognise that zero galvanometer current means the potential across R equals the 2 V cell's emf (the 2 V branch carries no current) → Write the current in the 10 V loop through the 400 ohm resistor and R in series as 10/(400+R) → Set the drop across R, 10R/(400+R), equal to 2 V and solve for R
why it is trickyThe work is reading the null condition as equal potentials across R and the 2 V cell, then applying a series-circuit potential divider in the 10 V loop and solving one linear equation.

Q11Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)
branchnot applicable (Physics has no branches)
chapterAlternating Current; Electromagnetic Waves medium confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(omega C) rising as frequency falls, so the current through the capacitor drops, and that current equals the displacement current between the plates.
subtopic 1Variation of Reactance with Frequency
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(omega C) rising as the frequency falls, so the current (equal to the displacement current) through the capacitor decreases.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
An ac source is connected to a capacitor C. Due to decrease in its operating frequency
  1. Displacement current increases
  2. Displacement current decreases
  3. Capacitive reactance remains constant
  4. Capacitive reactance decreases
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Capacitive reactance is XC = 12π f C.
  2. As f decreases, XC increases, ruling out options 3 and 4.
  3. Conduction current I = VXC therefore decreases.
  4. The displacement current between the plates equals the conduction current, Id = I, so Id decreases, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(2*π*f*C) and see that it rises as frequency falls (rules out options 3 and 4) → Apply I = V/X_C to see that the conduction current in the circuit falls → Recall that the displacement current between the plates equals the conduction current in the leads, so it falls too
why it is trickyThe work is one reactance-frequency recall applied through Ohm's law for ac, linked to the fact that displacement current equals conduction current in a capacitor.

subtopic 1Continuous and Characteristic X-Ray Spectra
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer rests on the Duane-Hunt cutoff wavelength of the continuous X-ray spectrum, lambda_min = hc/eV, so lambda_min is proportional to 1/V.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The minimum wavelength of X-rays produced by an electron accelerated through a potential difference of V volts is proportional to
  1. 1V
  2. 1V
  3. V2
  4. V
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. The shortest wavelength comes from an electron that gives all of its kinetic energy eV to a single photon: eV = hcλmin.
  2. λmin = hceV, so λmin ∝ 1V, which is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
11 highagree
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the Duane-Hunt limit: all of the electron's energy eV goes into one photon, eV = hc/λ_min, so λ_min = hc/(eV) and read off the dependence on V
why it is trickyThe work is a single recall of the cut-off wavelength relation λ_min = hc/eV for bremsstrahlung X-rays, from which the V-dependence is read directly.
the catchOption 2 (1/sqrt(V)) is the V-dependence of the accelerated electron's own de Broglie wavelength, a trap for a candidate who confuses the two wavelengths; it does not raise the band.

A full wave rectifier circuit consists of two p-n junction diodes, a centre-tapped transformer, capacitor and a load resistance. Which of these components remove the ac ripple from the rectified output?

  2. Capacitor
  3. Load resistance

  1. The capacitor placed across the load acts as a filter: it charges to the peak voltage and discharges slowly through the load between peaks, removing the ac ripple, so the answer is option 2.

subtopic 1Errors in Measurement

why this subtopicThe answer depends on propagation of relative errors in a product/quotient (dρ/ρ = dm/m + 2dr/r + dl/l = 0.5% + 0.67% + 0.4% ≈ 1.6%), which is taught under errors in measurement.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A metal wire has mass (0.4 ± 0.002) g, radius (0.3 ± 0.001) mm and length (5 ± 0.02) cm. The maximum possible percentage error in the measurement of density will nearly be
  1. 1.3%
  2. 1.6%
  3. 1.4%
  4. 1.2%
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Density of a wire: ρ = mπ r2 l.
  2. Maximum fractional error: Δ ρρ = Δ mm + 2Δ rr + Δ ll.
  3. Δ mm = 0.0020.4 = 0.005 = 0.5%.
  4. 2Δ rr = 2 × 0.0010.3 = 0.00667 = 0.667%.
  5. Δ ll = 0.025 = 0.004 = 0.4%.
  6. Δ ρρ = 0.5% + 0.667% + 0.4% = 1.567% ≈ 1.6%, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict

difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsWrite density as ρ = m / (π r2 l), so relative error = dm/m + 2 dr/r + dl/l → Compute each fractional error: mass 0.002/0.4, radius 0.001/0.3 (doubled for r2), length 0.02/5 → Add the percentage contributions and pick the nearest option
why it is trickyThe work is expressing density in terms of mass, radius and length, applying the error-propagation rule with the power of 2 on radius, and summing three percentage errors.
the catchOne option is exactly the result of forgetting to double the radius error (r appears squared in the volume).

A football player is moving southward and suddenly turns eastward with the same speed to avoid an opponent. The force that acts on the player while turning is
  1. Along northward
  2. Along north-east
  3. Along south-west
  4. Along eastward

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Take east as ˆı and north as ˆȷ; initial velocity →v1 = -vˆȷ (southward).
  2. Final velocity →v2 = vˆı (eastward, same speed).
  3. Δ→v = →v2 - →v1 = vˆı + vˆȷ.
  4. →F ∝ Δ→p = mΔ→v, which is directed at 45° between east and north, i.e. north-east, option 2.

subtopic 1Calculation of Gravitational Potential
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer -16Gm/R comes from summing the gravitational potentials of the two masses at the null point (R/4 from m), a gravitational potential calculation.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Two bodies of mass m and 9m are placed at a distance R. The gravitational potential on the line joining the bodies where the gravitational field equals zero, will be (G = gravitational constant)
  1. -12GmR
  2. -16GmR
  3. -20GmR
  4. -8GmR
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. At the null point a distance x from m: Gmx2 = G(9m)(R-x)2.
  2. Taking square roots: R - x = 3x, so x = R4.
  3. Distance from 9m is R - R4 = 3R4.
  4. V = -GmR/4 - G(9m)3R/4 = -4GmR - 12GmR.
  5. V = -16GmR, which is option 2.

Q36Physics
subjectPhysics
streamnot applicable (Physics has no streams)

chapterKinematics high confidence
why this chapterThe answer comes from applying the third equation of motion (v^2 = u^2 - 2as) under constant retardation, so the remaining 3 cm gives a total of 27 cm.
subtopic 1Third Equation of Motion
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer comes from applying v^2 = u^2 - 2as under uniform retardation (u^2/9 = u^2 - 2a(24) gives a further 3 cm, so 27 cm total), which is the third equation of motion.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A bullet from a gun is fired on a rectangular wooden block with velocity u. When bullet travels 24 cm through the block along its length horizontally, velocity of bullet becomes u3. Then it further penetrates into the block in the same direction before coming to rest exactly at the other end of the block. The total length of the block is
  1. 24 cm
  2. 28 cm
  3. 30 cm
  4. 27 cm
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Assume uniform retardation a inside the block and apply v2 = u2 - 2as to the first 24 cm.
  2. u2 - (u3)2 = 2a(24) ⇒ 8u29 = 48a.
  3. 2a = 8u29 × 24 = u227.
  4. For the remaining distance x the bullet goes from u3 to rest: (u3)2 = 2a x.
  5. u29 = u227 x ⇒ x = 3 cm.
  6. Total length L = 24 + 3 = 27 cm, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsApply v2 = u2 - 2as over the first 24 cm (u to u/3) to express the uniform retardation in terms of u → Apply v2 = u2 - 2as again over the remaining segment (u/3 to 0) with the same retardation to find the extra penetration → Add the extra penetration to 24 cm to get the total block length
why it is trickyTwo linked applications of the third kinematic equation under an assumed constant retardation, followed by adding the two distances.

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. P is the centre of the semicircle, and A, B are the feet of the perpendiculars from P to the two semi-infinite straight wires, each at distance R.
  2. Semicircular arc (current from A down the left side to B): Barc = μ0 i4R, directed out of the page by the right-hand rule.
  3. Upper semi-infinite wire (current rightward, P below it, ending at A): B1 = μ0 i4π R, into the page.
  4. Lower semi-infinite wire (current leftward, P above it, starting at B): B2 = μ0 i4π R, into the page.
  5. Net: B = μ0 i4R - 2μ0 i4π R = μ0 i4R[1-2π], out of the page, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
22 highagree
difficultyhard · 4 steps
stepsRecall the field at the centre of a semicircular arc, μ0 i / 4R, and find its direction from the arc's current sense by the right-hand rule → Recognise that P lies on the perpendicular through the end of each straight wire, so each semi-infinite wire contributes μ0 i / 4piR → Find the direction of each straight wire's field at P and see that both wires point the same way, opposite to the arc's field → Combine the opposing contributions as μ0 i/4R - 2 μ0 i/4piR and assign the net direction from the larger (arc) term
why it is trickyThe work is superposing three Biot-Savart contributions (one semicircular arc and two semi-infinite wires ending in line with P), getting each direction from the right-hand rule, and subtracting the wire terms from the arc term.
the catchThe options pair each magnitude with both directions, so a candidate who ignores the straight wires (or adds rather than subtracts them) or slips on one right-hand-rule direction lands exactly on a distractor.

  4. 40 cm
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. The slab of n2 = 1.6 with the lens of n1 = 1.5 inside acts as three thin lenses in contact in air: plano-concave (n2), biconvex (n1), plano-concave (n2).
  2. Biconvex lens: 1f1 = (1.5-1)(120+120) = 120 cm-1.
  3. Each plano-concave lens: 1f2 = (1.6-1)(1∞-120) = -3100 cm-1.
  4. Lenses in contact: 1F = 120 - 2×3100 = 5100-6100 = -1100 cm-1.
  5. F = -100 cm, which is option 2.

why it is trickyThe work is recognising the slab-with-embedded-lens as three separate thin lenses in contact, then two lens-maker calculations with correct sign conventions and a sum of powers.

Q53Chemistry / physical
subjectChemistry
streamphysical
branchnot applicable (Chemistry has no branches)
chapterStates of Matter high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the pressure-volume relationship at constant temperature (Boyle's Law), a gas law taught in States of Matter.
subtopic 1Gas Laws
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on Boyle's law (P proportional to 1/V at constant T, with steeper isotherms at higher T), which is taught under Gas Laws.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which amongst the following options are correct graphical representation of Boyle’s Law?
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · medium
  1. Boyle's law at constant n and T: PV = nRT, so P = nRT·1V.
  2. A plot of P against 1V is a straight line through the origin with slope nRT.
  3. Higher T gives a larger slope, so with T3 > T2 > T1 the T3 line is steepest; option 1 shows exactly this.
  4. Option 2 draws P vs 1V as hyperbolas, which is wrong.
  5. Option 3 is P vs T at constant V (Gay-Lussac's law), not Boyle's law.
  6. Option 4 is P vs V hyperbolas, but the outermost curve is labelled T1 although T3 > T2 > T1; the outermost isotherm should be T3, so the labelling is wrong.
  7. Only option 1 is a correct Boyle's law graph.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
11 mediumagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall Boyle's law (PV = constant at fixed T) and so identify the expected graph shapes: P vs V a rectangular hyperbola, P vs 1/V a straight line through the origin → Use PV = nRT to fix the temperature ordering of isotherms: higher T gives a steeper P-1/V line and a P-V hyperbola lying farther from the axes, then check the T labels on each P-V and P-1/V plot against that ordering → Reject the P vs T plot because it holds V constant (Gay-Lussac's law), not T, even though its isochore ordering is internally consistent
why it is trickyThe work is recalling the Boyle's-law graph shapes and then checking, via PV = nRT, whether each plot's isotherm labels run in the right temperature order and whether the plot holds T constant at all.
the catchTwo distractors look right at a glance: one P-V plot has the correct hyperbola shape but reversed temperature labels, and the P-T plot is a physically correct graph of a different gas law.

subtopic 1Activation Energy
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicBoth the assertion (a reaction can have zero activation energy) and the reason (definition of activation energy as energy needed to reach the threshold value) depend on the concept of activation energy.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion A and the other is labelled as Reason R : In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :
Assertion AA reaction can have zero activation energy.
Reasons RThe minimum extra amount of energy absorbed by reactant molecules so that their energy becomes equal to threshold value, is called activation energy.
  1. Both A and R are true and R is NOT the correct explanation of A
  2. A is true but R is false
  3. A is false but R is true
  4. Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · medium
  1. Activation energy is Ea = Ethreshold - Ereactants; if reactant molecules already possess the threshold energy, Ea = 0, so A is true.
  2. R states the standard definition of activation energy as the extra energy needed to reach the threshold value, so R is true.
  3. The definition in R does not account for why Ea can be zero, so R is not the correct explanation of A, which is option 1.

streamphysical
branchnot applicable (Chemistry has no branches)
chapterThe Solid State high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on ccp having 8 tetrahedral voids per 4 atoms, so A occupying 1/3 of them gives A2B3 (x + y = 5), which is close packing and voids in the solid state.
subtopic 1Close Packing, Tetrahedral and Octahedral Voids and Formula of Compounds
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on B in ccp giving 4 atoms with 8 tetrahedral voids, so A occupying 1/3 of them gives A2B3 and x + y = 5.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A compound is formed by two elements A and B. The element B forms cubic close packed structure and atoms of A occupy 1/3 of tetrahedral voids. If the formula of the compound is AxBy, then the value of x + y is in option
  1. 4
  2. 3
  3. 2
  4. 5
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Let the number of B atoms in the ccp lattice be N.
  2. A ccp lattice has 2N tetrahedral voids.
  3. A occupies 13 of them: nA = 13(2N) = 2N3.
  4. A : B = 2N3 : N = 2 : 3, so the formula is A2B3.
  5. x + y = 2 + 3 = 5, which is option 4.

why it is trickyRecall of the tetrahedral-void count per ccp atom, then one fraction-and-ratio application to fix the formula subscripts, then a sum.

subjectChemistry

chapterChemical Bonding and Molecular Structure high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the molecular orbital energy-level ordering for N2 (pi2p below sigma2p due to s-p mixing), which is molecular orbital theory in chemical bonding.
subtopic 1Energy Level Diagram for Molecular Orbitals
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the energy ordering of molecular orbitals for N2 (pi 2p below sigma 2p_z due to s-p mixing), which is the MO energy level diagram.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The correct order of energies of molecular orbitals of N2 molecule, is
  1. σ 1s < σ*1s < σ 2s < σ*2s < σ 2pz < (π 2px = π 2py) < (π*2px = π*2py) < σ*2pz
  2. σ 1s < σ*1s < σ 2s < σ*2s < σ 2pz < σ*2pz < (π 2px = π 2py) < (π*2px = π*2py)
  3. σ 1s < σ*1s < σ 2s < σ*2s < (π 2px = π 2py) < (π*2px = π*2py) < σ 2pz < σ*2pz
  4. σ 1s < σ*1s < σ 2s < σ*2s < (π 2px = π 2py) < σ 2pz < (π*2px = π*2py) < σ*2pz
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. For molecules up to N2 (total electrons ≤ 14), 2s-2p mixing raises the energy of σ 2pz above the degenerate π 2px = π 2py pair.
  2. So the order is σ 1s < σ*1s < σ 2s < σ*2s < (π 2px = π 2py) < σ 2pz < (π*2px = π*2py) < σ*2pz, which is option 4.

Amongst the following the total number of species NOT having eight electrons around central atom in its outer most shell, is NH3, AlCl3, BeCl2, CCl4, PCl5 :

  2. 4
  3. 1
  4. 3
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. NH3: N has 3 bond pairs + 1 lone pair = 8 electrons (octet).
  2. AlCl3: Al has 3 bond pairs = 6 electrons (incomplete octet).
  3. BeCl2: Be has 2 bond pairs = 4 electrons (incomplete octet).
  4. CCl4: C has 4 bond pairs = 8 electrons (octet).
  5. PCl5: P has 5 bond pairs = 10 electrons (expanded octet).
  6. Species not having 8 electrons: AlCl3, BeCl2, PCl5 = 3, which is option 4.

why it is trickyThe work is an electron count around the central atom for five molecules, a sort into octet, incomplete octet and expanded octet, and a count of the ones that are not eight; this is direct recall of the NCERT octet-rule exceptions applied five times.
the catchSmall traps: a candidate who thinks only of electron-deficient species may leave out the expanded octet of PCl5, and one who treats AlCl3 as its dimer Al2Cl6 may count it as having an octet. Each slip changes the count by one, which lands on a neighbouring option.

The element expected to form largest ion to achieve the nearest noble gas configuration is
  1. F
  2. N
  3. Na
  4. O
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. To reach the nearest noble gas configuration (Ne) the elements form N3-, O2-, F- and Na+, which all have 10 electrons.
  2. For isoelectronic ions, radius falls as nuclear charge rises: Z = 7 < 8 < 9 < 11.
  3. So N3- > O2- > F- > Na+; the largest ion comes from N, which is option 2.

the catchNa is the largest neutral atom among the options, which tempts a candidate who compares atomic rather than ionic size, although Na+ is actually the smallest ion.

chapterElectrochemistry high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the relation ΔrG = -nFE_cell and the intensive nature of cell potential versus the extensive nature of ΔrG, which is taught in electrochemistry (cell potential and Gibbs energy).
subtopic 1Nernst Equation
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the relation between Gibbs energy and cell EMF (ΔrG = -nFEcell), with ΔrG extensive and Ecell intensive, which is taught as 'Electrochemical Cell and Gibbs Energy of the Reaction' under the Nernst equation section.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Given below are two statements: one is labelled as Assertion A and the other is labelled as Reason R
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below
Assertion AIn equation ΔrG = -nFEcell, value of ΔrG depends on n.
Reasons REcell is an intensive property and ΔrG is an extensive property.
  1. Both A and R are true and R is NOT the correct explanation of A
  2. A is true but R is false
  3. A is false but R is true
  4. Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · medium
  1. Δr G = -nFEcell, where n is the number of moles of electrons transferred in the reaction as written, so Δr G changes with n: A is true.
  2. Ecell does not depend on the amount of reactants (intensive), while Δr G scales with the amount of reaction (extensive): R is true.
  3. Because Ecell is fixed but Δr G must scale with amount, the factor n carries that dependence, so R correctly explains A, which is option 4.

why it is trickyTwo conceptual recalls from electrochemistry thermodynamics followed by one linking judgement on whether the intensive/extensive distinction accounts for the n-dependence.

solver reasoning: E_cell is intensive and Delta_rG is extensive, so Delta_rG = -nFE_cell scales with n (moles of electrons); R explains why A holds.

Which amongst the following options is the correct relation between change in enthalpy and change in internal energy?
  1. Δ H = Δ U + Δ ngRT
  2. Δ H - Δ U = -Δ nRT
  3. Δ H + Δ U = Δ nR

The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

  1. By definition H = U + PV, so Δ H = Δ U + Δ (PV).
  2. For gases at constant T, PV = nRT gives Δ (PV) = Δ ng RT.
  3. Hence Δ H = Δ U + Δ ng RT, which is option 1.

subtopic 1Reactions of Ethers
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on cleavage of benzyl phenyl ether by HI, which gives benzyl iodide and phenol, a reaction of ethers.

Consider the following reaction : [reaction scheme figure] Identify products A and B.
  1. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · A OCc1ccccc1 C7H8O · B Ic1ccccc1 C6H5I
    A =, -CH2OH and B =, -I
  2. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · A ICc1ccccc1 C7H7I · B Oc1ccccc1 C6H6O
    A =, -CH2I and B =, -OH
  3. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · A Cc1ccccc1 C7H8 · B Ic1ccccc1 C6H5I
    A =, -CH3 and B =, -I

    A =, -CH3 and B =, -OH

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Protonation of the ether oxygen by HI gives C6H5CH2-+OH-C6H5.
  2. The aryl C-O bond has partial double-bond character and phenyl cations do not form, so cleavage occurs at the benzyl CH2-O bond, which gives a stable benzyl carbocation.

Which of the following statements are INCORRECT? Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
AAll the transition metals except scandium form MO oxides which are ionic.
BThe highest oxidation number corresponding to the group number in transition metal oxides is attained in Sc2O3 to Mn2O7.
CBasic character increases from V2O3 to V2O4 to V2O5.
DV2O4 dissolves in acids to give VO43- salts.

subtopic 1Reactions of Alcohols
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the ease of acid-catalysed dehydration of alcohols (carbocation stability), which is a reaction of alcohols.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which amongst the following be most readily dehydrated under acidic conditions?
  1. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · CC(O)CC(C)O C5H12O2
  2. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · CC(C(O)CO)[N+](=O)[O-] C4H9NO4
  3. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · CC(O)CCC(C)[N+](=O)[O-] C6H13NO3
  4. drawn by the pipeline from SMILES (RDKit) · CC(O)CC(C)[N+](=O)[O-] C5H11NO3
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · medium
  1. Read the options: (1) CH3CH(OH)CH2CH(OH)CH3; (2) CH3CH(NO2)CH(OH)CH2OH; (3) CH3CH(NO2)CH2CH2CH(OH)CH3; (4) CH3CH(NO2)CH2CH(OH)CH3.
  2. In (2) the α-H on the C-NO2 carbon is acidic and lies on the carbon adjacent to C-OH.
  3. Loss of H2O across these two carbons gives CH3C(NO2)=CH-CH2OH, a C=C conjugated with NO2, which is stabilised.
  4. In (3) and (4) the NO2 carbon is not adjacent to C-OH, and (1) has no activating group, so no conjugated alkene forms; hence option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
32 mediumsolved differs from official
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that acid-catalysed dehydration of an alcohol goes through a carbocation, so the ease of dehydration follows how stable that carbocation is → For each of the four structures (read from the question image), find the carbocation that forms when the OH leaves and note whether an NO2 group sits near it → Compare the four: the electron-withdrawing (-I) effect of NO2 destabilises a nearby carbocation, and the closer it is the stronger the effect, so pick the structure whose carbocation has no NO2 near it
why it is trickyThe work is to apply the carbocation (E1) mechanism, find the cation site in four structures, and rank them by how strongly the -I effect of NO2 acts at different distances; this is one recall plus one application, with no deeper insight needed.

solver reasoning: In option 2 the C-H alpha to NO2 sits next to the C-OH, so loss of water gives a nitroalkene conjugated with NO2 (acidic alpha-H, conjugated stable product); the others cannot form such a conjugated alkene.

READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The reaction that does NOT take place in a blast furnace between 900 K to 1500 K temperature range during extraction of iron is :
  1. FeO + CO → Fe + CO2
  2. C + CO2 → 2CO
  3. CaO + SiO2 → CaSiO3
  4. Fe2O3 + CO → 2FeO + CO2
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. In the blast furnace, the 500-800 K zone has 3Fe2O3 + CO → 2Fe3O4 + CO2, Fe3O4 + 4CO → 3Fe + 4CO2 and Fe2O3 + CO → 2FeO + CO2.

  3. So Fe2O3 + CO → 2FeO + CO2 does not take place at 900-1500 K, which is option 4.

Q94Chemistry / organic
subjectChemistry
streamorganic
branchnot applicable (Chemistry has no branches)
chapterGeneral Organic Chemistry medium confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on applying Huckel's (4n+2) pi-electron rule to decide which compounds/ions are aromatic, which is the Aromaticity topic of General Organic Chemistry.
subtopic 1Aromaticity
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on counting species that satisfy Huckel's (4n+2) pi-electron rule, which is the criterion for aromaticity.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Consider the following compounds/species: [figure: structures i.–vii.] The number of compounds/species which obey Huckel’s rule is ______.
  1. 6
  2. 2
  3. 5
  4. 4
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. i. Naphthalene: planar, cyclic, conjugated with 10 π electrons = 4(2)+2, so it obeys Huckel's rule.
  2. ii. Cyclopentadienyl anion: 4 π electrons from two C=C plus the lone pair gives 6 π electrons = 4(1)+2, so it obeys.
  3. iii. Cyclobutadiene: 4 π electrons = 4n with n=1, antiaromatic, does not obey.
  4. iv. Cyclopropenyl anion: 2 π from C=C plus the lone pair gives 4 π electrons = 4n, does not obey.
  5. v. Cyclopropenyl cation: 2 π electrons = 4(0)+2, so it obeys.
  6. vi. Cyclooctatetraene: 8 π electrons = 4n and tub-shaped (non-planar), does not obey.
  7. vii. Anthracene: 14 π electrons = 4(3)+2, so it obeys.
  8. Species obeying: i, ii, v, vii, so the count is 4, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultyhard · 5 steps
stepsFor each of the seven species, check that it is cyclic, planar and fully conjugated (decide whether the charged carbon or lone pair joins the π system, and whether the 8-membered ring stays planar) → Count the π electrons in each, including the lone pair on the anions and the empty p orbital on the cation → Treat the fused polycycles (naphthalene, anthracene) by counting the whole periphery π electrons (10 and 14) → Test each count against 4n+2, telling apart the 4-π species (cyclobutadiene, cyclopropenyl anion, cyclooctatetraene with 8) from the 2/6/10/14-π ones → Tally the species that pass and match the total to an option
why it is trickyOne rule (Huckel 4n+2 with the planarity/conjugation conditions) has to be applied independently to seven structures, each needing its own π-electron count, and then tallied, so a single misjudgement changes the answer.
the catchPaired near-identical traps: the cyclopropenyl anion and cation differ only in the sign of the charge (4 vs 2 pi electrons), and cyclooctatetraene looks like a conjugated ring but is non-planar with 8 pi electrons; miscounting one of these lands exactly on a neighbouring option (5 or 6).

  1. 3, 8, 1
  2. 1, 8, 3
  3. 8, 1, 3
  4. 1, 3, 8
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Oxidation states: Cr in Cr2O72- is +6, in Cr3+ is +3; S in SO32- is +4, in SO42- is +6.
  2. Electrons gained per Cr2O72-: 2 × 3 = 6; electrons lost per SO32-: 2.
  3. Equate electrons: 6a = 2b ⇒ b = 3a; take a = 1, b = 3.
  4. Charge balance: left -2 - 6 + c, right 2(+3) + 3(-2) = 0, so c - 8 = 0 ⇒ c = 8.
  5. Check oxygen: left 7 + 9 = 16, right 12 + c2 = 12 + 4 = 16. Balanced.
  6. a, b, c = 1, 3, 8, which is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
44 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsAssign oxidation-state changes: Cr goes +6 to +3 (6 electrons gained per dichromate, a=1 since product carries 2a Cr), S goes +4 to +6 (2 electrons lost per sulfite) → Equate electrons gained and lost to fix b relative to a → Balance charge (or O/H) on both sides to fix c, then match the ordered triple to the options
why it is trickyThe work is a standard ion-electron/oxidation-number balance of a dichromate-sulfite reaction in acid: electron bookkeeping for b, then a charge or hydrogen balance for c.

subtopic 1Allotropes, Oxyacids and Oxides of Sulphur
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the bond structures (S-OH, S=O, S-O-S, S-O-O-S) of the oxoacids of sulphur.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Match List-I with List – II : List-I (Oxoacids of Sulphur) List-II (Bonds) Choose the correct answer from the options given below.
APeroxodisulphuric acidITwo S-OH, Four S=O, One S-O-S
BSulphuric acidIITwo S-OH, One S=O
CPyrosulphuric acidIIITwo S-OH, Four S=O, One S-O-O-S
DSulphurous acidIVTwo S-OH, Two S=O
  1. A–III, B–IV, C–I, D–II
  2. A–I, B–III, C–IV, D–II
  3. A–III, B–IV, C–II, D–I
  4. A–I, B–III, C–II, D–IV
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Peroxodisulphuric acid H2S2O8: HO-S(O2)-O-O-S(O2)-OH has two S-OH, four S=O and one S-O-O-S, so A → III.
  2. Sulphuric acid H2SO4: (HO)2S(=O)2 has two S-OH and two S=O, so B → IV.
  3. Pyrosulphuric acid H2S2O7: HO-S(O2)-O-S(O2)-OH has two S-OH, four S=O and one S-O-S, so C → I.
  4. Sulphurous acid H2SO3: (HO)2S=O has two S-OH and one S=O, so D → II.
  5. A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
11 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall the structure of peroxodisulphuric acid and identify the S-O-O-S linkage, matching it to the bond set containing the peroxide bridge → Recall the structure of sulphurous acid (one S=O, two S-OH) and match it to the bond set with a single S=O → Use these two pairings to eliminate options, confirming with the sulphuric acid (two S=O) and pyrosulphuric acid (S-O-S) structures
why it is trickyThe work is recalling the structural formulae of four sulphur oxoacids, counting their S-OH, S=O and bridging bonds, and matching them, though two distinctive recalls are enough to eliminate options.

Identify the correct statements:
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
ADetrivores perform fragmentation.
BThe humus is further degraded by some microbes during mineralization.
CWater soluble inorganic nutrients go down into the soil and get precipitated by a process called leaching.
DThe detritus food chain begins with living organisms.
EEarthworms break down detritus into smaller particles by a process called catabolism.
  1. B, C, D only
  2. C, D, E only
  3. D, E, A only
  4. A, B, C only

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. A: detritivores such as earthworms break detritus into smaller particles (fragmentation), so A is true.
  2. B: humus is degraded very slowly by some microbes and releases inorganic nutrients by mineralization, so B is true.
  3. C: water-soluble inorganic nutrients go down into the soil horizon and get precipitated as unavailable salts by leaching, so C is true.
  4. D: the detritus food chain begins with dead organic matter (detritus), not living organisms, so D is false.
  5. E: earthworms breaking detritus into smaller particles is fragmentation; catabolism is enzymatic degradation by bacteria and fungi, so E is false.

Q128Biology / botany

chapterSexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the ploidy of embryo sac cells after double fertilisation (haploid synergids, diploid zygote, triploid primary endosperm nucleus), which is taught in Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants.
subtopic 1Double Fertilisation
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the ploidy of the structures in an embryo sac after double fertilisation: haploid synergids, diploid zygote from syngamy and triploid primary endosperm nucleus from triple fusion.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
In angiosperm, the haploid, diploid and triploid structures of a fertilized embryo sac sequentially are :
  1. Antipodals, synergids, and primary endosperm nucleus
  2. Synergids, Zygote and Primary endosperm nucleus
  3. Synergids, antipodals and Polar nuclei
  4. Synergids, Primary endosperm nucleus and zygote
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (2)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Synergids are cells of the egg apparatus, formed from the haploid megaspore, so they are haploid (n).
  2. Syngamy fuses the egg (n) with one male gamete (n): zygote = n + n = 2n (diploid).
  3. Triple fusion joins two polar nuclei (n + n) with the second male gamete (n): PEN = 3n (triploid).
  4. The order haploid, diploid, triploid is synergids, zygote, primary endosperm nucleus, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict

why it is trickyThe work is recalling the ploidy of four or five embryo-sac structures after double fertilization and then checking which option lists them in the stated order.

subtopic 1Commensalism and Ammensalism
subtopic 2Mutualism
why subtopic 1The answer depends on the sign notation of population interactions, and half the matching (commensalism +/0 and amensalism -/0) is the content of the Commensalism and Ammensalism topic.
why subtopic 2The match also requires knowing that mutualism is the +/+ interaction, which is taught under the Mutualism topic, so the question genuinely combines it with the commensalism/amensalism pair.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Match List I with List II : Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
AMutualismI+(A), O(B)
BCommensalismII–(A), O(B)
CAmensalismIII+(A), –(B)
DParasitismIV+(A), +(B)
  1. A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III
  2. A-IV, B-III, C-I, D-II
  3. A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II
  4. A-IV, B-II, C-I, D-III
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Mutualism benefits both species: +(A), +(B), so A → IV.
  2. Commensalism benefits one species and leaves the other unaffected: +(A), 0(B), so B → I.
  3. Amensalism harms one species and leaves the other unaffected: -(A), 0(B), so C → II.
  4. Parasitism benefits the parasite and harms the host: +(A), -(B), so D → III.
  5. A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III matches option 1.

Which of the following statements are correct about Klinefelter’s Syndrome? Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
AThis disorder was first described by Langdon Down (1866).

CThe affected individual is short statured.
DPhysical, psychomotor and metal development is retarded.

  2. B and E only

printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader

Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

BAdhesionIIMutual attraction among water molecules
CSurface tensionIIIWater loss in liquid phase
DGuttationIVAttraction towards polar surfaces
  1. A – IV, B – III, C – II, D – I
  2. A – III, B – I, C – IV, D – II

The publisher's solutionprinted answer (4)

subtopic 1Packaging of DNA Helix
subtopic 2not assigned (one subtopic chosen)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on how DNA is packaged: negatively charged DNA held with positively charged proteins in the prokaryotic nucleoid and wrapped around a positively charged histone octamer to form the eukaryotic nucleosome.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Given below are two statements: In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

Statement IIIn eukaryotes, the negatively charged DNA is wrapped around the positively charged histone octamer to form nucleosome.
  1. Both Statement I and Statement II are false.
  2. Statement I is correct but Statement II is false.
  3. Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is true.
  4. Both Statement I and Statement II are true.
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. DNA is negatively charged because of its phosphate groups, so the phrase 'positively charged DNA' in Statement I is wrong; in prokaryotes the DNA is held by positively charged proteins in the nucleoid. Statement I is incorrect.
  2. In eukaryotes, the negatively charged DNA wraps around a positively charged histone octamer to form the nucleosome. Statement II is true.
  3. Statement I incorrect and Statement II true is option 3.

the catchStatement I swaps the charges of DNA and its associated proteins while keeping the rest of the NCERT sentence intact, so a candidate reading for keywords (nucleoid, prokaryotes) can accept it without noticing the swap.

why subtopic 2Statements A, C and E test Prophase I substages (tetrad formation in zygotene not leptotene, terminalisation in diakinesis not pachytene, crossing over between non-sister chromatids), and judging them false is needed to reject options 2, 3 and 4.

subtopic 1Animal Breeding

READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which one of the following is NOT an advantage of inbreeding?
  1. It exposes harmful recessive genes but are eliminated by selection.
  2. Elimination of less desirable genes and accumulation of superior genes takes place due to it.
  3. It decreases the productivity of inbred population, after continuous inbreeding.
  4. It decreases homozygosity.
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (3)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · low
  1. Inbreeding is mating of closely related individuals for 4-6 generations, so it increases homozygosity.
  2. Exposing harmful recessive genes so selection removes them (option 1) is an advantage.
  3. Eliminating less desirable genes and accumulating superior genes (option 2) is an advantage.
  4. Continued inbreeding reduces fertility and productivity, i.e. inbreeding depression (option 3), which is a disadvantage.
  5. Option 4 is factually false (inbreeding increases homozygosity), so it is also not an advantage; the intended disadvantage is option 3.

why it is trickyThe work is recalling the NCERT list of what inbreeding does, then sorting four statements into advantage, disadvantage, and factually wrong.
the catchTwo options are not advantages: option 3 (inbreeding depression, a real disadvantage) and option 4 (false, since inbreeding increases homozygosity). The candidate has to decide whether the question wants the false statement or the true disadvantage.

why it is trickyThe work is one textbook recall applied to five separate items, each classified as excretory or reproductive, and then matched to a combination option.
the catchOne option leaves out a single item from the correct excretory set, so a candidate who recalls the list only partly gets caught by it.

printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Select the correct statements with reference to chordates. Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
APresence of a mid-dorsal, solid and double nerve cord.
BPresence of closed circulatory system.
CPresence of paired pharyngeal gill slits.
DPresence of dorsal heart
ETriploblastic pseudocoelomate animals.
  1. B and C only
  2. B, D and E only
  3. C, D and E only
  4. A, C and D only
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (1)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. A is false: the chordate nerve cord is dorsal, hollow and single, not solid and double.
  2. B is true: chordates have a closed circulatory system.
  3. C is true: chordates have paired pharyngeal gill slits.
  4. D is false: the chordate heart is ventral.
  5. E is false: chordates are triploblastic coelomates, not pseudocoelomates.
  6. Only B and C are correct, which is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
11 highagree
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall the NCERT chordate vs non-chordate comparison table (nerve cord dorsal/hollow/single vs ventral/solid/double; heart ventral vs dorsal; gill slits present; closed circulation) → Check each of the five statements A-E against that table, catching the mixed-up qualifiers in A (dorsal but solid and double) and D (dorsal heart), and recalling that chordates are coelomate, not pseudocoelomate (E) → Match the set of accepted statements to the option combinations
why it is trickyThe work is recalling one NCERT comparison table and checking five statements against it, two of which splice a chordate word with a non-chordate feature, then matching the surviving set to an option.
the catchStatements A and D each pair a chordate-sounding word ('mid-dorsal', 'dorsal') with a non-chordate feature, so a candidate reading quickly accepts them; option 4 is built from exactly that misreading.
